--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (C and F), we first need to identify all the paths between these two nodes.

1. **Node C Connections:**
   - C is connected to B, G, and O.

2. **Node F Connections:**
   - F is connected to S, E, J, and T.

3. **Paths from C to F:**
   - C → B → E → F
   - C → B → S → F
   - C → G → I → H → P → Q → J → F
   - C → G → I → H → P → Q → J → T → F

We now analyze the paths:
- The path C → B → E → F can be cut by removing the edge (B, E).
- The path C → B → S → F can be cut by removing the edge (B, S).
- The path C → G → I → H → P → Q → J → F can be cut by removing the edge (G, I).
- The path C → G → I → H → P → Q → J → T → F can be cut by removing the edge (G, I) or any intermediate edge in this path.

Notice that by removing edges (B, E) and (B, S), we effectively cut off both C → B → E → F and C → B → S → F. Additionally, removing edge (G, I) will cut C → G → I → H → P → Q → J → F and C → G → I → H → P → Q → J → T → F.

Thus, the minimum set of edges to remove is:

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(B, E), (B, S), (G, I)].

   - From N: N → B → H → I → P → Q
   - From N: N → O → G → H → Q

3. **Identify critical nodes for removal:**

4. **Determine the minimal cut set:**
   - Removing B and H will not disconnect all paths.
   - Removing an additional node such as C or O would disconnect the remaining paths.

By analyzing the graph, we conclude that the minimal set of nodes to remove to ensure N and Q are completely disconnected is [B, H, C].

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed from the graph to completely disconnect the two black nodes (A and T), we need to examine the paths connecting these nodes. Here are the steps to identify these paths:

1. **Identify the two black nodes**: A and T.
2. **Find all paths between A and T**:
   - A -> E -> B -> G -> H -> T
   - A -> E -> B -> G -> J -> H -> T
   - A -> S -> B -> G -> H -> T
   - A -> S -> B -> G -> J -> H -> T
   - A -> E -> F -> B -> G -> H -> T
   - A -> E -> F -> B -> G -> J -> H -> T
   - A -> E -> F -> B -> G -> C -> J -> H -> T
   - A -> S -> B -> G -> C -> J -> H -> T

3. **Determine the bottleneck (common edges) in the paths**: Any edge that appears in all paths will be a candidate for removal.

4. **Identify the minimal set of edges to remove**:
   - Removing the edge (G, H) will disconnect paths from G to H but there are still other paths through J.
   - Removing the edge (G, J) will disconnect paths through J but there are still other paths through C.
   - Removing the edge (B, G) will disconnect multiple paths but there are still paths through F and B.

By performing this analysis we see:
- Removing (B, G) and (G, H) together ensures that all paths between A and T are disconnected, as no other paths remain without going through either B to G or G to H directly or indirectly.

**Conclusion**:
Edges to remove: 2,  Cut Set: [(B, G), (G, H)].
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 3, 'cut_set': [[['A', 'B'], ['A', 'E'], ['A', 'S']], [['T', 'F'], ['T', 'B'], ['T', 'G']]]}

The task requires identifying the minimum set of nodes whose removal would disconnect the two black nodes (E and T). We need to go through the graph and determine the paths between these two nodes. By identifying the key nodes that are part of these paths, we can determine the minimum cut set.

Let's analyze the graph:

1. **Node E is connected to nodes:** A, B, F, S, and J.
2. **Node T is connected to nodes:** B, G, and S.

We need to disconnect E and T by removing the minimal set of nodes.

### Paths between E and T:
1. **Path 1:** E -> B -> T
2. **Path 2:** E -> S -> T
3. **Alternative Paths involving intermediate nodes:**
   * B connects further to A, F, H, J, and S.
   * S connects to C, F, and G.
   * G connects to C, R, S, I, and Q.

### Analysis of bottleneck paths:

By blocking any of the paths, we can disconnect the nodes. The essential nodes to block any path between them:
- **Node B**: Direct connection between E and T.
- **Node S**: Another direct path between E and T.
- **Analyzing node traces near intersections:**
  By removing B and S, we disrupt multiple paths, but there are still paths through other nodes like A, F, C, G, R, etc.
  Necessary to analyze the additional nodes involved in multiple paths cross-sectioned by node overlaps:
  - **Node R**: Though not directly connected to E or T but forms auxiliary connections.
  - **Node F**: Forms an important connecting bridge between multiple paths including nodes E and T intersections.

### Final Cut Set Plausibility:
To identify a minimal cut set satisfying complete disconnection:
From closely evaluating the overlapping paths proportion said:
**Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [B, S, F]**

Nodes B and S are wrapping across those paths if removing them together:
F disrupts the interior overlaps. F serves minimum preservation criterion while reducing path scope collectively.

Complete disconnection minus minimal exceedances -- the minimal cut set identified.

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [B, S, F]

   - Remove the edge between B and H.
   - Remove the edge between B and G.
   - Remove the edge between R and I.

    - From `I`, it connects to `Q` and `H`.
    - From `Q`, it connects to `O`.
    - From `H`, it connects to `B`, `G`, and `T`.
    - From `T`, it connects to `B`, `S`, `E`, and `F`.
    - From `B`, it connects to `C`, `E`, `F`, and `S`.
    - From `C`, it connects to `G` and `N`.
    - From `N`, it connects to `S` and `C`.
    - From `G`, it connects to `H` and `S`.

    - The paths from `R` to `P` must be traced through these nodes.
    - `P` connects to `Q` and `I`.

3. **Identify bottleneck edges**:
    - The paths from `R` to `P` are:
        - `R` -> `J` -> `I` -> `P`
        - `R` -> `J` -> `I` -> `Q` -> `P`
        - `R` -> `C` -> `G` -> `H` -> `B` -> `T` -> `E` -> `S` -> `N` -> `C` -> `R`, and so on.
    - Upon further inspection, `I` and `Q` are critical nodes because they provide direct connections to `P`.

4. **Determine the minimal cut set**:
    - Removing the edges `(R, J)` and `(I, P)` cuts the paths directly connected to `P`.
    - Removing the edge `(J, I)` also helps in ensuring `R` is disconnected from these paths.
    - Finally, removing the edge `(Q, P)` ensures that any path through `Q` to `P` is cut.

Thus, the minimum number of edges to remove to completely disconnect `R` and `P` is 4.

**Edges to remove: 4, Cut Set: [(R, J), (I, P), (J, I), (Q, P)].**
